No semantic approval or runtime execution."},"text_dictionary":{"25c8af0c9eea81c91a3c783e9cd7386eab47579d4bb34d628f210acce45dd2dd":"# Import behavior\nThe importer accepts integer IDs and nonempty values, rejecting duplicate IDs within a batch. It validates the whole batch before passing it once to the local Store. Validation failure preserves existing records. Valid imports preserve all supplied rows. Network services, concurrent writers and storage-outage rollback are outside this contract. Tests inspect full before/after records through the public import boundary. The project owner decides behavior changes; implementation and orientation do not redefine this contract.\n","cdb40b88631336ae1b8b916c9ea4ecbee04306fd7c60c44ef4a60fafee826aa1":"# Project workflow\nOwner-approved direction precedes living Design and independent Design Review. Stable delivery allocation and independent Delivery Review precede planned implementation. Each slice receives independent Code Review, followed by fresh whole-change review and separate final Verify. Manual artifact requests remain isolated. Mutable work is tracked only under explicit current project authority; no stored lifecycle format is adopted in this synthetic project.\n","227dff27bda3d0103a4d28d247c604eab930bcaeb13e2804edb85c853aea5aca":"# Ledger Garden project rules\n\n## Project purpose\nThis project serves volunteer groups keeping useful records locally. VISION.md owns direction when present; otherwise the explicit project owner supplies direction.\n\n## Source authority\nExplicit owner instructions govern the authorized scope. These rules govern engineering, followed by the owning Design, approved plan, tests and implementation. Current practice does not override authority.\n\n## Requirements\nObservable behavior MUST have an owning Design and independently reviewable expected outcomes before implementation. docs/design/import.md owns import behavior.\n\n## Tests\nChanges SHOULD establish concrete proof first. Bug fixes MUST preserve the original failing proof and add regression protection. Configured commands MUST NOT be reported as executed.\n\n## Architecture\nThe import boundary MUST validate all rows before calling the local Store. Data ownership and migrations remain with docs/design/import.md.\n\n## Security and privacy\nAgents MUST preserve private records and MUST NOT publish secrets. Network access and any external write require explicit owner approval.\n\n## Compatibility\nChanges MUST explain format compatibility, migration and recovery. Existing readable exports remain supported unless an approved direction explicitly changes that guarantee.\n\n## Verification\nRun the project-owned applicable commands before completion claims; report actual results and limitations. Local success MUST NOT be called hosted CI success.\n\n## Review\nA contributor MUST NOT independently approve their own contribution. Required independent review precedes reliance; final verification remains distinct.\n\n## Documentation\nAffected living Designs and user-facing guidance MUST agree with changed behavior. Link detailed procedures at their owners rather than duplicating them here.\n\n## Agent behavior\nPreserve unrelated work, disclose missing evidence and material assumptions, and avoid unrelated refactoring. A map reports observed state, not future architecture or execution commitments.\n\n## Standard workflow and manual use\nFollow docs/workflow.md for the selected standard lifecycle. Isolated manual skills remain bounded; writing an artifact alone grants no approval, continuation or external permission.\n","62c4a87619d2cdd9379f41602b27e5eb1f18be15be9655155c50aad134c88cdd":"# Ledger Garden\n\n## Pitch\n\nLedger Garden is a local-first record organizer for small volunteer groups. It keeps their lists useful without an account by importing, checking and storing records on their own machine.\n\n## What makes this different\n\nUnlike hosted spreadsheets, Ledger Garden works without a network account. Groups retain direct control of their records, accepting fewer collaboration features in return.\n\n\n## Who it is for\n\nVolunteer coordinators who maintain modest lists on one shared computer and need readable, exportable records.\n\n## Who it is not for\n\nLarge organizations needing simultaneous remote editing, cloud administration or live analytics.\n\n## What it commits to\n\nOrdinary list work remains available offline. Invalid import input leaves existing records unchanged. Users can inspect and export every stored record.\n\n## What it refuses to be\n\nIt does not become an advertising service, mandatory cloud account or remote surveillance tool.\n\n## What would prove this wrong\n\nThe direction fails if ordinary list work needs network access, groups cannot recover readable exports, or import validation silently loses existing records.\n\n","a394c70c0de7a3aa6702c16dae5fec0733566bc6318aeff73e7faaf03bc6ebb0":"# Strategic positioning\n\n`VISION.md` is the canonical project-vision artifact. This supporting rationale explains how the vision was derived and does not independently override it.\n\n## Project category\n\nLocal-first record organizer.\n\n## Primary user\n\nSmall volunteer groups maintaining lists on a shared computer.\n\n## Primary pain\n\nHosted tools require accounts and reliable connectivity for simple record work.\n\n## Primary promise\n\nUseful, readable records remain under the group’s local control.\n\n## Core mechanism\n\nImport, validate and store locally, with inspectable export.\n\n## Alternatives\n\nHosted spreadsheets offer richer simultaneous collaboration. Plain text offers less structured validation.\n\n## Tradeoff\n\nLocal control and offline operation take priority over remote collaboration.\n\n## Compatibility surfaces\n\nPython, JSON files and command-line invocation are implementation compatibility choices, not the product category.\n\n## Refusals\n\nNo mandatory cloud account, advertising or remote surveillance.\n\n## Falsifiability\n\nOrdinary use requiring network access, unrecoverable exports or silent record loss contradicts the promise.\n","6fd6b3c849774876af20b7ce6a6ab72babcc189be8af6fd61dc084b74aa3c8f4":"# Ledger Garden\n![Build](badge.svg)\n\n<!-- outside-prefix: preserve exactly -->\n<!-- vision:start -->\nLedger Garden is a local-first record organizer for small volunteer groups. It keeps their lists useful without an account by importing, checking and storing records on their own machine.\n\nUnlike hosted spreadsheets, Ledger Garden works without a network account. Groups retain direct control of their records, accepting fewer collaboration features in return.\n\nFor volunteer coordinators who maintain modest lists on one shared computer and need readable, exportable records.\n\nSee [the vision](VISION.md) for goals, non-goals and falsifiability.\n<!-- vision:end -->\n<!-- outside-suffix: preserve exactly -->\n## Install\nRead AGENTS.md.  \n## Usage\nRun the local application.\n","4e843880105915ecbe64bd49b5d80779ef2386b967d0688bbb6354d1b46e5c51":"# Ledger Garden project map\n\n## Map metadata\n\n- Map status: current\n- Scope: repository\n- Baseline: supplied-tree-2026-09-18\n- Last reviewed: 2026-09-18\n- Coverage: src/app.py, src/store.py, package.json, tests/test_store.py, AGENTS.md and docs/design/import.md\n- Exclusions: Hosted/deployment state; no network or test command was executed.\n- Parent map: not-applicable\n- Known gaps: none\n\n## Purpose and scope\n\nOrientation of the inspected local importer and Store. Users can inspect and export every stored record.\n\n## What it refuses to be\n\nIt does not become an advertising service, mandatory cloud account or remote surveillance tool.\n\n## What would prove this wrong\n\nThe direction fails if ordinary list work needs network access, groups cannot recover readable exports, or import validation silently loses existing records.\n\n","9a882ac328c73da0a04bfc7e69ebc4c3b3ea2247921c78c31a3a03887e8f2fa9":"# Ledger Garden\n![Build](badge.svg)\n\n<!-- outside-prefix: preserve exactly -->\n<!-- vision:start -->\nLedger Garden is a local-first record organizer for small volunteer groups. It keeps their lists useful without an account by importing, checking and storing records on their own machine.\n\n## What makes this different\n\nUnlike hosted spreadsheets, Ledger Garden works without a network account. Groups retain direct control of their records, accepting fewer collaboration features in return.\n\n\n## Who it is for\n\nVolunteer coordinators who maintain modest lists on one shared computer and need readable, exportable records.\n\n## Who it is not for\n\nLarge organizations needing simultaneous remote editing, cloud administration or live analytics.\n\n## What it commits to\n\nOrdinary list work remains available offline. Invalid import input leaves existing records unchanged. Users can inspect and export every stored record.\n\n## What it refuses to be\n\nIt does not become an advertising service, mandatory cloud account or remote surveillance tool.\n\n## What would prove this wrong\n\nThe direction fails if ordinary list work needs network access, groups cannot recover readable exports, or import validation silently loses existing records.\n\n","56c5223573a896984d202f85631242479d13d36742bab34ab20513bd7abfbf01":"# Strategic positioning\n\n`VISION.md` is the canonical project-vision artifact. This supporting rationale explains how the vision was derived and does not independently override it.\n\n## Project category\n\nLocal-first record organizer.\n\n## Primary user\n\nSmall volunteer groups and neighborhood associations maintaining lists on a shared computer.\n\n## Primary pain\n\nHosted tools require accounts and reliable connectivity for simple record work.\n\n## Primary promise\n\nUseful, readable records remain under the group’s local control.\n\n## Core mechanism\n\nImport, validate and store locally, with inspectable export.\n\n## Alternatives\n\nHosted spreadsheets offer richer simultaneous collaboration. Plain text offers less structured validation.\n\n## Tradeoff\n\nLocal control and offline operation take priority over remote collaboration.\n\n## Compatibility surfaces\n\nPython, JSON files and command-line invocation are implementation compatibility choices, not the product category.\n\n## Refusals\n\nNo mandatory cloud account, advertising or remote surveillance.\n\n## Falsifiability\n\nOrdinary use requiring network access, unrecoverable exports or silent record loss contradicts the promise.\n","eebcfd619a9cdd29b234f956be95ea08242f8082ce538017dd03069cb7820585":"# Ledger Garden\n![Build](badge.svg)\n\n<!-- outside-prefix: preserve exactly -->\n<!-- vision:start -->\nLedger Garden is a local-first record organizer for small volunteer groups and neighborhood associations. All twelve applicable rule families are supplied in full and preserved; temporary M1 Friday deadline is scheduling context, not a governing principle.","528907ee835d8b9ef1d560ecc487145f3b2fae3c2801b68c6c35876634ef4252":"# Ledger Garden project rules\n\n## Project purpose\nThis project serves volunteer groups keeping useful records locally. VISION.md owns direction when present; otherwise the explicit project owner supplies direction.\n\n## Source authority\nExplicit owner instructions govern the authorized scope. These rules govern engineering, followed by the owning Design, approved plan, tests and implementation. Current practice does not override authority.\n\n## Requirements\nObservable behavior MUST have an owning Design and independently reviewable expected outcomes before implementation. docs/design/import.md owns import behavior.\n\n## Tests\nChanges SHOULD establish concrete proof first. Bug fixes MUST preserve the original failing proof and add regression protection. Configured commands MUST NOT be reported as executed. Evidence MUST identify exact inputs, observed results and limitations so an independent reviewer can evaluate the claimed outcome.\n\n## Architecture\nThe import boundary MUST validate all rows before calling the local Store. Data ownership and migrations remain with docs/design/import.md.\n\n## Security and privacy\nAgents MUST preserve private records and MUST NOT publish secrets. Network access and any external write require explicit owner approval.\n\n## Compatibility\nChanges MUST explain format compatibility, migration and recovery. Existing readable exports remain supported unless an approved direction explicitly changes that guarantee.\n\n## Verification\nRun the project-owned applicable commands before completion claims; report actual results and limitations. Local success MUST NOT be called hosted CI success.\n\n## Review\nA contributor MUST NOT independently approve their own contribution. Required independent review precedes reliance; final verification remains distinct.\n\n## Documentation\nAffected living Designs and user-facing guidance MUST agree with changed behavior. Link detailed procedures at their owners rather than duplicating them here.\n\n## Agent behavior\nPreserve unrelated work, disclose missing evidence and material assumptions, and avoid unrelated refactoring. A map reports observed state, not future architecture or execution commitments.\n\n## Standard workflow and manual use\nFollow docs/workflow.md for the selected standard lifecycle. Isolated manual skills remain bounded; writing an artifact alone grants no approval, continuation or external permission.\n","93f9076e435d0a3d19bc81ef0e371d2278d9279b18ca1410df396d7d2325a6d3":"All identities are SHA-256 of literal packet content. No runtime flow was execution-observed. Complete integer/duplicate validation required by docs/design/import.md is not established by this implementation excerpt.\n\n## Data flow\n\nObserved in src/store.py: JSON serialization writes records.json. Inferred: a process working directory controls that relative destination. No persistent fixture was executed.\n\n## External boundaries\n\nNot observed in the mapped scope. The supplied importer and Store use local modules and filesystem only; no deployment evidence was supplied.\n\n## Test map\n\nConfigured in package.json: python -m unittest discover tests. tests/test_store.py checks only example row values; it does not prove full persistence or the complete Design. No command was executed and no pass is claimed.\n\n## CI and release map\n\nNot observed in the mapped scope. No CI/release/deployment configuration or hosted observations were supplied; none is claimed.\n\n## Architecture rules observed\n\nAGENTS.md requires whole-batch validation before Store. src/app.py is one observed instance of validate-before-save order, with narrower validation than the Design; the map does not approve that discrepancy.\n\n## Risk areas\n\nObserved implementation excerpt lacks integer/duplicate validation promised by docs/design/import.md. All unlisted project bytes, especially neighbor.txt and docs/notes.txt, remain preserved.\nSame map-only request and conflict evidence.","21b6065f9eb96dab32d72f4ede5359328c8277bc8fc1ba40de22da3c27155609":"# Ledger Garden\n\n## Pitch\n\nLedger Garden is a cloud-first record organizer for small volunteer groups. It keeps their lists useful without an account by importing, checking and storing records on their own machine.\n\n## What makes this different\n\nUnlike hosted spreadsheets, Ledger Garden requires a network account. Groups retain direct control of their records, accepting fewer collaboration features in return.\n\n\n## Who it is for\n\nVolunteer coordinators who maintain modest lists on one shared computer and need readable, exportable records.\n\n## Who it is not for\n\nLarge organizations needing simultaneous remote editing, cloud administration or live analytics.\n\n## What it commits to\n\nOrdinary list work remains available offline. Invalid import input leaves existing records unchanged. Users can inspect and export every stored record.\n\n## What it refuses to be\n\nIt does not become an advertising service, mandatory cloud account or remote surveillance tool.\n\n## What would prove this wrong\n\nThe direction fails if ordinary list work needs network access, groups cannot recover readable exports, or import validation silently loses existing records.\n\n","76559962759838a2d5228693eb255dc443df2e1b7c7a4a8b37990d550335eac7":"All identities are SHA-256 of literal packet content.
